\partbox{The group}
\section{Names and structure}\label{s:identification}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.0.1}{0.1}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.2}{2}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.12}{12}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.20}{20} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.28}{28}.
{\small\setlength{\fillwidth}{\textwidth}\addtolength{\fillwidth}{-2\tabcolsep}\setlength{\colwidth}{0pt}\cellmax{\texttt{commutator\symbol{95}subgroup}}\cellmax{\texttt{gap\symbol{95}smallgroup\symbol{95}id}}\cellmax{\texttt{hermann\symbol{95}mauguin}}\cellmax{\texttt{abstract\symbol{95}type}}\cellmax{\texttt{product\symbol{95}form}}\cellmax{\texttt{su2\symbol{95}subgroup}}\cellmax{\texttt{centralizer}}\cellmax{\texttt{normalizer}}\cellmax{\texttt{cover\symbol{95}of}}\cellmax{\texttt{center}}\cellmax{\texttt{symbol}}\cellmax{\texttt{order}}\addtolength{\fillwidth}{-\colwidth}\setlength{\colwidth}{0pt}\cellmax{direct: $\grp{2C_{12}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{C_{i}}$; $\grp{2C_{12}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{C_{i}}\secondclass$; $\grp{2S_{12}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{C_{i}}$; $\grp{2S_{12}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{C_{i}}\secondclass$; $\grp{2C_{4h}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{C_{3}}$; $\grp{2C_{4}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{S_{6}}$; $\grp{2C_{4}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{S_{6}}\secondclass$; $\grp{2S_{4}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{S_{6}}$; $\grp{2S_{4}}$${}\dirprod{}$$\grp{S_{6}}\secondclass$}\cellmax{$\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$, index 2 in the normalizer}\cellmax{$\grp{2C_{12h}}$, index 1}\cellmax{$\grp{2C_{12}}$, index 2}\cellmax{$\grp{C_{1}}$, index 48}\cellmax{$\Cyc{24}\dirprod \Cyc{2}$}\cellmax{[48, 23]}\cellmax{$\grp{2C_{12h}}$}\cellmax{$\dmark\hmname{12/m}$}\cellmax{$\grp{2D_{\infty h}}$}\cellmax{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}\cellmax{48}\fillcap\begin{longtable}{@{}l>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{\fillwidth}@{}}\toprule
\texttt{symbol} & \href{2C12h.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{12h}}$}\\
\texttt{cover\symbol{95}of} & \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}\\
\grouprule{0pt}{\lightrulewidth}
\texttt{abstract\symbol{95}type} & $\Cyc{24}\dirprod \Cyc{2}$\\
\texttt{order} & 48\\
\texttt{gap\symbol{95}smallgroup\symbol{95}id} & [48, 23]\\
\grouprule{0pt}{\lightrulewidth}
\texttt{su2\symbol{95}subgroup} & \href{2C12.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{12}}$}, index 2\\
\texttt{normalizer} & \href{2Dinfh.pdf}{$\grp{2D_{\infty h}}$}\\
\texttt{centralizer} & \href{2Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$}, index 2 in the normalizer\\
\texttt{product\symbol{95}form} & direct: \href{2C12.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{12}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S2.pdf}{$\grp{C_{i}}$}; \href{2C12.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{12}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S2b.pdf}{$\grp{C_{i}}\secondclass$}; \href{2S12.pdf}{$\grp{2S_{12}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S2.pdf}{$\grp{C_{i}}$}; \href{2S12.pdf}{$\grp{2S_{12}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S2b.pdf}{$\grp{C_{i}}\secondclass$}; \href{2C4h.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{4h}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{C3.pdf}{$\grp{C_{3}}$}; \href{2C4.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{4}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S6.pdf}{$\grp{S_{6}}$}; \href{2C4.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{4}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S6b.pdf}{$\grp{S_{6}}\secondclass$}; \href{2S4.pdf}{$\grp{2S_{4}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S6.pdf}{$\grp{S_{6}}$}; \href{2S4.pdf}{$\grp{2S_{4}}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S6b.pdf}{$\grp{S_{6}}\secondclass$}\\
\texttt{center} & \href{2C12h.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{12h}}$}, index 1\\
\texttt{commutator\symbol{95}subgroup} & \href{C1.pdf}{$\grp{C_{1}}$}, index 48\\
\grouprule{0pt}{\lightrulewidth}
\texttt{hermann\symbol{95}mauguin} & $\dmark\hmname{12/m}$\\
\bottomrule\end{longtable}}
\leavevmode\label{n:element.inversion}\label{n:group.preimage}The group $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ is the preimage in $\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}, whose document sets the fields that this table does not repeat (\texttt{cover\symbol{95}of}): its type, family, geometry, crystal classes and the names of its \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/O3.pdf}{$\grp{O(3)}$}-conjugacy class. The field \texttt{su2\symbol{95}subgroup} is $\grp{2C_{12h}} \cap \grp{SU(2)}$, the largest subgroup of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ in $\grp{SU(2)}$ (its elements of parity $+1$), and \texttt{normalizer} and \texttt{centralizer} are taken in $\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$. The fields \texttt{center} and \texttt{commutator\symbol{95}subgroup} are classes of the table of section~\ref{s:subgroups}.
\needspace{103pt}
\section{The cover}\label{s:cover}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.20.1}{20.1}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.1}{21.1} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.2}{21.2}.
\leavevmode\label{n:group.pin.minus}\label{n:group.su2}\label{n:local.pin.sign}\label{n:matrix.covered}\label{n:matrix.lift.su2}\label{n:index.bar}The point group $\grp{C_{12h}}$ has the preimage $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ in $\grp{Pin^{-}(3)} = \grp{SU(2)} \dirprod \{+1, -1\}$, whose element $(U, \varepsilon)$ covers $\varepsilon R(U) \in \grp{O(3)}$, where $U$ covers the rotation $R(U)$. The preimage is the double group of $\grp{C_{12h}}$, with the $2 \cdot 24 = 48$ elements $(g, 0) = (U(g), \det g)$ and $(g, 1) = (-U(g), \det g)$, $g \in \grp{C_{12h}}$, where $U(g)$ is the canonical lift of $g$ to $\grp{SU(2)}$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.1}{21.1}). \label{n:function.cocycle}With $U(h)\,U(g) = (-1)^{c(g,h)}\,U(gh)$, $gh$ = first $g$, then $h$, the product is
\begin{equation}\label{e:product}(g, b)(h, b') = \bigl(gh,\ b + b' + c(g, h) \bmod 2\bigr).\end{equation}
\leavevmode\label{n:mark.bar}The covering map $(g, b) \mapsto g$ has kernel $\{\elt{E}, \elt{\barred{E}}\}$, where $\elt{\barred{E}} = (\elt{E}, 1)$ is central of order 2. \label{n:element.named}\label{n:mark.lift}The barred partner $\elt{\barred{X}}$ of an element $\elt{X}$ of $\grp{C_{12h}}$ is $(\elt{X}, 1)$, and the lift of the inversion, $\elt{\lift{i}} = (1, -1)$, is central of order 2.
\needspace{122pt}
\section{Symmetry operations}\label{s:elements}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.4}{4}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.5.1}{5.1} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21}{21}.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.element}\label{n:element.g}Element $k$ of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ is the pair $(g, b)$ of the element $g$ of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} of index $\lfloor k/2\rfloor$ in its document and $b = k - 2\lfloor k/2\rfloor$. The pair $(g, 0)$ is the canonical lift of $g$, and $(g, 1)$ is its barred partner (section~\ref{s:cover}). \label{n:matrix.element.pin}\label{n:matrix.element}The matrix $M(k)$ of the element is the transpose of its $\grp{SU(2)}$ part $(-1)^{b}\,U(g)$, and the matrices compose as the matrices $P(g)$ of the elements of $\grp{C_{12h}}$:
\begin{equation}\label{e:mprod}M(k)\,M(k') = M(kk'),\qquad kk' = \text{first } k\text{, then } k'.\end{equation}
\leavevmode\label{n:angle.omega}\label{n:vector.axis}\label{n:vector.pole}That part is the lift $\lift{R}_{\vv}(\omega)$, $0 \le \omega < 4\pi$, of the rotation part $(\det g)\,g$ of the element, about the axis $\vv$ of $g$ (a dash for none, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.1}{21.1}). \label{n:angle.euler.alpha}\label{n:angle.euler.beta}\label{n:angle.euler.gamma}\label{n:vector.coordinates}The Euler angles of the rotation part are $(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)$: by $\alpha$ about $z$, then by $\beta$ about $y$, then by $\gamma$ about $z$ (fixed axes). \label{n:local.euler.sign}The sign $s$ of the lift satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{e:euler}(-1)^{b}\,U(g) = s\,\expe^{-\iunit\gamma\pauli{z}/2}\,\expe^{-\iunit\beta\pauli{y}/2}\,\expe^{-\iunit\alpha\pauli{z}/2}.\end{equation}
\leavevmode\label{n:index.element.inverse}\label{n:head.sym}\label{n:head.ord}A row gives the index $k$ of the element, its symbol (sym, barred for a barred partner), its order (ord) and the index $k^{-1}$ of its inverse. \label{n:head.class}The class of the element is set with its size before its symbol (section~\ref{s:classes}).
The table lists the canonical lifts $(g, 0)$, of even $k$. The barred partner $(g, 1) = \elt{\barred{E}}\,(g, 0)$, of index $k + 1$, has the barred symbol and class, the same $\det$, $(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)$ and axis, $\omega + 2\pi$ modulo $4\pi$, the sign $-s$ and the matrix $-M(k)$. Its order and inverse (\texttt{elements.items}) are read from $\omega + 2\pi$ and the multiplication table.
\leavevmode\label{n:constant.root}\label{n:index.root.order}Exact values are rationals, square roots, cosines and sines of rational multiples of $\pi$, and the constants
\begin{equation}\label{e:constants}\rootunity{n} = \expe^{2\pi\iunit/n}\quad(n = 3, 8, 12, 24),\end{equation}
where $\iunit$ is the imaginary unit and $\expe$ is the base of the natural logarithm.

\section{Conjugacy classes}\label{s:classes}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.5}{5} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.22}{22}.
A class of $\grp{C_{12h}}$ lifts to one class of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ that contains both lifts of its elements (its size doubled, no bar) or to two classes listed next to each other (section~\ref{s:cover}). The two are the class of the canonical lifts $(g, 0)$ of these elements and that of their barred partners $(g, 1)$.
\leavevmode\label{n:head.size}\label{n:head.classof}A row is a class of elements $g$, with their number (size), their order (ord) and the classes of $g^{-1}$ and $g^{2}$. It also gives the angle $\omega$ of the lift of the rotation part of $g$ (section~\ref{s:elements}) and the axis $\vv$ of a member (a dash for none).

\section{Fundamental domain}\label{s:fundamental_domain}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.17}{17} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.3}{21.3}.
An object with symmetry $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ placed by $\lift{Q} \in \grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ is the same when placed by $g\lift{Q}$ with $g \in \grp{2C_{12h}}$. \label{n:operator.cosets}Thus its orientations are the cosets $\grp{2C_{12h}}\lift{Q} = \{g\lift{Q} : g \in \grp{2C_{12h}}\}$, the points of the space $\grp{2C_{12h}}\backslash\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$. \label{n:operator.volume}Volumes are those of the metric that the covering map carries to that of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/O3.pdf}{$\grp{O(3)}$} (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.17}{17}), so that $\vol\grp{SU(2)} = 16\pi^2$ and $\vol\grp{Pin^{-}(3)} = 32\pi^2$, twice those of $\grp{SO(3)}$ and $\grp{O(3)}$. A finite group has the counting measure, $\vol\grp{2C_{12h}} = |\grp{2C_{12h}}| = 48$.
\leavevmode\label{n:set.lens}As $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ contains $\elt{\barred{E}}$, a coset $\grp{2C_{12h}}\lift{Q}$ contains both lifts of every element of a coset of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} in $\grp{O(3)}$, and $\grp{2C_{12h}}\backslash\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ is $\grp{C_{12h}}\backslash\grp{O(3)}$, $\grp{C_{12}}\backslash\grp{SO(3)}$, the lens space $\lensspace{24}{1}$. A cell's rotations stand here for their canonical lifts (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.1}{21.1}). \label{n:set.cell}\label{n:set.fundamental}Thus the cell $\fcell$, with its facets, and the fundamental domain $\fdomain$ are those of the document of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}, in its section Fundamental domain. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{e:volume}\vol\fdomain = \tfrac{2}{3}\pi^2,\qquad \vol\grp{2C_{12h}}\cdot\vol\fdomain = 48\cdot \tfrac{2}{3}\pi^2 = 32\pi^2 = \vol\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}.\end{equation}

The group $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ acts on the directions through the covering map, so its orbifold is that of the document of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} (\texttt{orbifold}).
Forgetting the turn about a direction sends the orientation space $\grp{2C_{12h}}\backslash\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ onto that orbifold.
The harmonics on $\grp{2C_{12h}}\backslash\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ are those on $\grp{C_{12h}}\backslash\grp{O(3)}$ of the document of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} (\texttt{harmonics}), of integer $J$ since $\elt{\barred{E}}$ acts on $\irrJp{J}{p}$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.1}{25.1}) by $(-1)^{2J}$.
The invariant forms of section~\ref{s:invariants} are the functions on the singularity of type A\textsubscript{23}, the quotient of the spin kets by the matrices of the elements.
\needspace{171pt}
\partbox{Representations}
\section{Irreducible representations}\label{s:irreps}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.6}{6}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.7}{7} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.23}{23}.
\leavevmode\label{n:function.character}\label{n:irrep.Gamma}The character $\irchar{\Gamma}(g)$ of the irrep $\Gamma$ on the class of $g$ is given in section~\ref{s:character_table}. \label{n:index.dimension}The vector irreps ($\irchar{\Gamma}(\elt{\barred{E}}) = +d$, $d$ the dimension) are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}, on both lifts (\texttt{vector\symbol{95}irreps}), and the spinor irreps ($\irchar{\Gamma}(\elt{\barred{E}}) = -d$) follow below the rule. A spinor irrep is labeled by its dimension, $\irr{a}$ (1), $\irr{e}$ (2), $\irr{g}$ (4) or $\irr{i}$ (6), and a complex one by that of its complex-conjugate pair ($\irr{e}$ for dimension 1, $\irr{g}$ for dimension 2). The label carries the tag $\ptag{g}$ or $\ptag{u}$ of $\irchar{\Gamma}(\elt{\lift{i}}) = \pm d$ and the least half-integer $J$ at which the irrep occurs in $\irrJp{J}{+1}$ of $\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ for $\ptag{g}$, or in $\irrJp{J}{-1}$ for $\ptag{u}$ (section~\ref{s:subduction_parent}). Two that would share a label carry the prefixes 1 and 2: 1 has the character of the smaller argument in $[0, 2\pi)$ on the first class with unequal characters.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.frobenius}\label{n:head.irrep}\label{n:head.reality}A row is an irrep of dimension $d$, with its Frobenius--Schur indicator $\fsind = |\grp{2C_{12h}}|^{-1}\allowbreak\sum_{g\in\grp{2C_{12h}}}\irchar{\Gamma}(g^2)$, which gives its reality: real ($\fsind = +1$), pseudoreal ($\fsind = -1$) or complex ($\fsind = 0$, paired with its conjugate). \label{n:head.conjugate}The column conjugate gives that conjugate (a dash for the others). \label{n:head.over.real}Each irrep lies in the irrep over $\mathbb{R}$ of its row. \label{n:head.kernel}The kernel $\ker\Gamma$ is the subgroup of the elements mapped to the identity, named as in section~\ref{s:subgroups}. \label{n:head.image}The image is the group formed by the irrep's matrices, isomorphic to $\grp{2C_{12h}}/\ker\Gamma$. For a vector irrep it is that of the irrep of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}. For a spinor irrep of dimension 1 it is the cyclic group of the characters. For a spinor irrep of dimension 2 with its matrices in $\grp{SU(2)}$ it is the preimage $\grp{2H}$ formed by them. Otherwise it is the group of this catalogue isomorphic to it. The irreps are irreducible over $\mathbb{C}$. Over $\mathbb{R}$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.6.4}{6.4}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.7}{7} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.23.2}{23.2}), a real irrep is also an irrep in a basis with real matrices. The two irreps $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}$ of a complex-conjugate pair together form one irrep over $\mathbb{R}$ of dimension $2d$ and character $2\operatorname{Re}\irchar{\conjpair{1}\irr{e}}$, labeled without the prefix when they are vector irreps. A complex-conjugate pair of spinor irreps forms one irrep over $\mathbb{R}$ of dimension $2d$, named by the labels of its two members side by side, in irrep order. \label{n:mark.determinant}The boxed irrep is the determinant character of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}, of value $\det g$ on the element $(g, b)$.

\section{Character table}\label{s:character_table}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.6}{6}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.7}{7} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.23}{23}.

\section{Representation matrices}\label{s:irrep_matrices}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.9}{9} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.23.3}{23.3}.
\leavevmode\label{n:vector.ket.partner}\label{n:index.partner}\label{n:matrix.irrep}A basis $(\irket{\Gamma}{1}, \dots, \irket{\Gamma}{d})$ of the irrep $\Gamma$ obeys
\begin{equation}\label{e:partner}g\irket{\Gamma}{\intx} = \sum_\nu \irket{\Gamma}{\nu}\, \repD{\Gamma}_{\nu,\intx}(g);\end{equation}
the matrices of a vector irrep are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}, on both lifts. Row $k$ of each table gives $\repD{\Gamma}(g)$ of a spinor irrep for the canonical lift $g$ of even index $k$ (section~\ref{s:elements}), and $\repD{\Gamma}(g) = \irchar{\Gamma}(g)$. On the barred partner $\barred{g}$ of that lift, a spinor irrep has $\repD{\Gamma}(\barred{g}) = -\repD{\Gamma}(g)$.

\section{Direct products of irreps}\label{s:fusion}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.10}{10}.
\leavevmode\label{n:irrep.Gamma.factors}Every product $\Gamma_1\irrprod\Gamma_2$, equal to $\Gamma_2\irrprod\Gamma_1$, is one irrep, in row $\Gamma_1$, column $\Gamma_2$ for $\Gamma_1 \le \Gamma_2$ in irrep order.
{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}\begin{longtable}{@{}lcccccccccccc@{}}\toprule

\needspace{91pt}
\section{Clebsch--Gordan coefficients}\label{s:clebsch_gordan}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.10}{10}.
\leavevmode\label{n:function.cg.one}\label{n:vector.ket.one}Every irrep is one-dimensional, so each product $\Gamma_1\irrprod\Gamma_2$ is one irrep $\Gamma$ (the fusion rules of section~\ref{s:fusion}) and its coefficient is $\cgcone{\Gamma_1}{\Gamma_2}{\Gamma} = 1$: $\ket{\Gamma} = \ket{\Gamma_1}\ket{\Gamma_2}$ (\texttt{clebsch\symbol{95}gordan.\allowbreak products[]}).
The products of two vector irreps have the coefficients of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} (\texttt{vector\symbol{95}products}).
\needspace{171pt}
\partbox{Subgroups}
\section{Subgroup lattice}\label{s:subgroups}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.15}{15} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.28}{28}.
The diagram shows the conjugacy classes of subgroups, each joined to the classes of its maximal subgroups, and the orders increase upward. \label{n:index.conjugates.c}A normal subgroup is framed, and ${\times}c$ is the number $c > 1$ of conjugate subgroups. \label{n:group.H}A subgroup that contains $\elt{\barred{E}}$ is the preimage of its image in $\grp{O(3)}$, named $\grp{2H}$ for the image $\grp{H}$. Any other subgroup is a section over its image, named $\grp{H}$. The table lists the classes by order, then by the type of the image, a preimage before a section. \label{n:head.subgroup}\label{n:head.order}\label{n:head.conjugates}\label{n:head.normal}A row gives the representative $\grp{H}$ of a class of subgroups (subgrp), its order, the number of subgroups in the class (conjugates) and whether $\grp{H}$ is normal. \label{n:group.normalizer}\label{n:group.centralizer}\label{n:head.classes}It also gives the normalizer $\grp{N}_{\hairsp \grp{2C_{12h}}}(\grp{H})$ and the centralizer $\grp{C}_{\hairsp \grp{2C_{12h}}}(\grp{H})$ in $\grp{2C_{12h}}$, by their classes (\texttt{normalizer}, \texttt{centralizer}), and the classes of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ other than $\cls{E}$ met by $\grp{H}$ (classes, a dash for none). A subgroup's other names in its \href{Pinminus3.pdf}{$\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$}-conjugacy class follow $\samecls$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.20.2}{20.2}).

\needspace{103pt}
\section{Descent of symmetry}\label{s:subduction}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.12.2}{12.2}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.12.4}{12.4} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.28}{28}.
A column is a class of subgroups $\grp{H}$, in the order of the table of section~\ref{s:subgroups}. The row subgrp names it by its symbol, as in that table. The symbol links to the document of $\grp{H}$. The entry of row $\Gamma$ is $\restr{\Gamma}{\grp{H}}$, the restriction of $\Gamma$ along a monomorphism from the record of $\grp{H}$ onto the subgroup, as a sum of the irreps of $\grp{H}$. The monomorphism identifies the subgroup with its record, and $\grp{H}$ names both. The rows of the vector irreps of a preimage are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} (\texttt{vector\symbol{95}subduction}). The column of a section, which the record does not store, is that of its preimage with the spinor irreps renamed (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.27}{27}). \label{n:group.normalizer.o3}\label{n:group.centralizer.o3}\label{n:element.h}The record of $\grp{H}$ maps onto the subgroup in $\grpindex{\grp{N}}{\grp{C}}$ ways, $h \mapsto g h g^{-1}$ ($h \in \grp{H}$) for the $g \in \grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ that carry $\grp{H}$ to it. Here $\grp{N}$ and $\grp{C}$ are the normalizer and centralizer of $\grp{H}$ in \href{Pinminus3.pdf}{$\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$}. In the section Names and structure of the document of $\grp{H}$, the row centralizer gives $\grp{C}$ and the index $\grpindex{\grp{N}}{\grp{C}}$ (the index in the row normalizer is $\grpindex{\grp{N}}{\grp{H}}$). Two maps related by a conjugation in $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ give the same restriction. Thus the maps fall into $\grpindex{\grp{N}}{\grp{C}}/\grpindex{\grp{N}_{\hairsp \grp{2C_{12h}}}}{\grp{C}_{\hairsp \grp{2C_{12h}}}}$ classes, where $\grp{N}_{\hairsp \grp{2C_{12h}}}$ and $\grp{C}_{\hairsp \grp{2C_{12h}}}$ are the normalizer and centralizer of the subgroup in $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ (the table of section~\ref{s:subgroups}). \label{n:head.setting}A subgroup whose classes of maps give different tables has one column for each table: setting 1 for the stored monomorphism (\texttt{monomorphism}) and settings 2, 3, \dots{} for the \texttt{alternative\symbol{95}settings}. Each setting has the images of the generators of $\grp{H}$ (\texttt{setting}). \label{n:head.images}The row images gives, under each setting, each generator whose image differs between the settings, in their order, mapped to the class of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ that contains its image. Each generator is written by the symbol of its class (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.5.1}{5.1}), and where two share a symbol, a superscript number in brackets gives the generator's index in the document of $\grp{H}$. \label{n:irrep.Delta}Each other setting is setting 1 with each irrep $\Lambda$ of $\grp{H}$ replaced by $\Lambda$ composed with one automorphism of $\grp{H}$ induced by a conjugation in \href{Pinminus3.pdf}{$\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$} and by none in $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.12.1}{12.1}).

Read down a column, a table gives induction (Frobenius reciprocity): $\Gamma$ occurs in $\Ind{\grp{H}}{\grp{2C_{12h}}} \Lambda$ as often as $\Lambda$ occurs in the entry of row $\Gamma$. For every entry of a spinor irrep, the record holds the unitary matrix that carries $\restr{\Gamma}{\grp{H}}$ into the direct sum of the matrices of $\grp{H}$ (\texttt{subduction[].blocks}). For every class $\grp{H}$ it holds the left coset representatives (\texttt{transversal}). From both, $\Ind{\grp{H}}{\grp{2C_{12h}}} \Lambda$ and its intertwiners follow as in \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.12.4}{12.4}.
\needspace{190pt}
\partbox{Angular momentum}
\section{Subduction from \texorpdfstring{$\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$}{Pin\unichar{8315}(3)}}\label{s:subduction_parent}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.11}{11} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.1}{25.1}.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.J}\label{n:index.parity}\label{n:irrep.o3}The irrep $\irrJp{J}{p}$ of \href{Pinminus3.pdf}{$\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$} has angular momentum $J$ ($J = 0, \nicefrac{1}{2}, 1, \dots$) and parity $p$ ($\ptag{g}$ for $p = +1$, $\ptag{u}$ for $p = -1$). An element $(U, \varepsilon)$ acts on it as $\varepsilon^{[p = -1]}$ times $U$ acting on the kets of angular momentum $J$, and $\elt{\barred{E}}$ as $(-1)^{2J}$. \label{n:function.character.parent}\label{n:function.multiplicity}An element whose $\grp{SU(2)}$ part is $\lift{R}_{\vv}(\omega)$ (section~\ref{s:elements}) has the character
\begin{equation}\label{e:chi}\chi^{(J,p)}(g) = p^{\iverson{\det g = -1}}\sum_{m=-J}^{J} \expe^{\iunit m\omega},\end{equation}
and $b_\Gamma(J, p)$ is the multiplicity of $\Gamma$ in
\begin{equation}\label{e:parent}\restr{\irrJp{J}{p}}{\grp{2C_{12h}}}.\end{equation}
At integer $J$ only the vector irreps occur, and $b_\Gamma(J, p)$ is the multiplicity in the subduction from $\grp{O(3)}$ to \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} (\texttt{integer\symbol{95}J}). \label{n:index.increment}At half-integer $J$ only the spinor irreps occur: each table gives $b_\Gamma(J, p)$ for $J \le \nicefrac{41}{2}$, and $b_\Gamma(J + 12, p) = b_\Gamma(J, p) + \Delta_\Gamma$ for every $J$, where $\Delta_\Gamma$ is the last row of the table of parity $p$. A table leaves out irreps of multiplicity 0 at every $J$.
\leavevmode\label{n:operator.determinant.character}The determinant exchanges the parities: $\irrJp{J}{-1} = \irrJp{J}{+1} \irrprod \det$, so that $b_\Gamma(J, -1) = b_{\Gamma \irrprod \det_{\grp{2C_{12h}}}}(J, +1)$.
\needspace{82pt}

\section{Multiplicities and allowed \texorpdfstring{$m$}{m}}\label{s:multiplicities}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.14}{14} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.26}{26}.
\leavevmode\label{n:function.generating}\label{n:index.generating.variable}\label{n:function.generating.numerator}The multiplicity $b_\Gamma(J) = b_\Gamma(J, +1)$ is the number of blocks of $\Gamma$ at $J$, with the generating function
\begin{equation}\label{e:gf}H_\Gamma(t) = \sum_{J\ge1/2} b_\Gamma(J)\,t^{2J} = P_\Gamma(t)/((1-t^{2})(1-t^{24})),\end{equation}
summed over the half-integer $J$. \label{n:delimiter.floor}\label{n:local.floor.x}The floor $\lfloor x\rfloor$ is the greatest integer $\le x$. The listed residues are the $2m \bmod 24$ of the nonzero components of the vectors of $\Gamma$. The irreps that no block carries, $P_\Gamma(t) = 0$, are left out: the kets carry $\irrJp{J}{+1}$, and the blocks of $\irrJp{J}{-1}$ carry those irreps (section~\ref{s:bases}).
{\small{\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}\begin{longtable}{@{}llll@{\hspace{18pt}}llll@{}}\toprule
$\Gamma$ & $P_\Gamma(t)$ & $b_\Gamma(J)$ & $2m \bmod 24$ & $\Gamma$ & $P_\Gamma(t)$ & $b_\Gamma(J)$ & $2m \bmod 24$\\\midrule\endhead
\bottomrule\endfoot\endlastfoot
$\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t + t^{23}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+1}{24}\bigr\rfloor + 1$ & 1 & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{7} + t^{17}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+7}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+17}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 7\\*
$\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t + t^{23}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+1}{24}\bigr\rfloor + 1$ & 23 & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{7} + t^{17}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+7}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+17}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 17\\*
$\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{3} + t^{21}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+3}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+21}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 3 & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{9} + t^{15}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+9}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+15}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 9\\*
$\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{3} + t^{21}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+3}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+21}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 21 & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{9} + t^{15}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+9}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+15}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 15\\*
$\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{5} + t^{19}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+5}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+19}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 5 & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{11} + t^{13}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+11}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+13}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 11\\*
$\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{5} + t^{19}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+5}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+19}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 19 & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{g}}$ & $t^{11} + t^{13}$ & $\bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+11}{24}\bigr\rfloor + \bigl\lfloor\tfrac{2J+13}{24}\bigr\rfloor$ & 13\\
\bottomrule\end{longtable}}}

\section{Symmetry-adapted bases}\label{s:bases}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.14}{14} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.26}{26}.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.m}\label{n:vector.ket}The bases at integer $J$ are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} (\texttt{integer\symbol{95}J}), and at half-integer $J$ the kets $\jm{J}{m}$, $m = J, J-1, \dots, -J$, carry $\irrJp{J}{+1}$. \label{n:matrix.wigner}The Wigner matrix $\wignerD{J}$ of $\grp{SU(2)}$ satisfies $\wignerD{1/2}(U) = U$ on $\jm{1/2}{1/2}$, $\jm{1/2}{-1/2}$. The element $g$ of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ whose $\grp{SU(2)}$ part is $U$ acts as
\begin{equation}\label{e:action}g\,\jm{J}{m} = \sum_{m'} \jm{J}{m'}\; \conj{\wignerD{J}_{m m'}(U)},\end{equation}
so $\lift{R}_{z}(\omega)$ multiplies $\jm{J}{m}$ by $\expe^{\iunit m\omega}$, $0 \le \omega < 4\pi$, and $\elt{\barred{E}}$ by $-1$. \label{n:index.ketcoefficient}\label{n:vector.basis.symmetry}\label{n:index.ketweight}\label{n:index.total}A vector is listed by its coefficients $e_m$:
\begin{equation}\label{e:ket}v = \frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tot}}}\sum_m e_m\sqrt{F_m}\;\jm{J}{m},\qquad F_m = (J+m)!\,(J-m)!,\qquad \mathrm{tot} = \sum_m |e_m|^2F_m,\end{equation}
so every $e_m$ here is $\pm 1$.
\leavevmode\label{n:group.A}The subgroup $\grp{A}$ of the elements over $\grp{C_{\infty h}}$ acts diagonally on the kets, and $\grp{2C_{12h}} = \grp{A}$: every ket is a block. Blocks are ordered by $J$, then $|m|$, $+m$ before $-m$.
At the parity $p = -1$ a block keeps its kets, and its irrep $\Gamma$ becomes $\Gamma \irrprod \det_{\grp{2C_{12h}}}$: $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{g}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{u}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{7/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{9/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{g}}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{u}} \to \conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{11/2,\ptag{g}}$.
The bases have 132 blocks and 132 vectors at $J = \nicefrac{1}{2}, \nicefrac{3}{2}, \dots, \nicefrac{21}{2}$. \label{n:index.copy}\label{n:head.vector}A row gives a vector of a block at $J$: the block's irrep and copy index $\mult$, the partner index $\intx$ and the vector.

\partbox{Spinors and tensors}
\section{Spin--orbitals}\label{s:spin_orbitals}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.4}{25.4}.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.orbital.l}The states of an electron of orbital angular momentum $l$ and spin $\nicefrac{1}{2}$ are each given up to a positive factor. \label{n:local.spinor.uv}\label{n:local.function.f}They are written in the functions $f$ of the $\orbital{s}$, $\orbital{p}$ and $\orbital{d}$ shells of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$} (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.13}{13}) times the spin kets $u = \jm{1/2}{1/2}$ and $v = \jm{1/2}{-1/2}$. The record also holds the $\orbital{f}$ shell (\texttt{spin\symbol{95}orbitals}). An orbital level consists of the functions of degree $l$ that carry the irrep $\Gamma$ of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/C12h.pdf}{$\grp{C_{12h}}$}. With the spin, it splits into the spinor irreps $\Lambda$ of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$, those of the doublet of $\Gamma$ in the spin--orbit levels (section~\ref{s:spin_orbit}, first column). \label{n:head.state}Under the heading of its shell, a row gives $\Gamma$, a copy $\mult$ of $\Lambda$ (when there are several), its partner $\intx$ and the state. The normalized state (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.4}{25.4}) follows the partner law with the matrices of section~\ref{s:irrep_matrices}.

$\Gamma$ & $\Lambda$ & $\intx$ & state & $\Gamma$ & $\Lambda$ & $\intx$ & state\\\midrule\endhead

 & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{u}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{p}_{x} + \iunit{}\,\orbital{p}_{y}\right)u$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{2\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{x^2-y^2} + 2\iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{xy}\right)v$\\*

\needspace{103pt}
\section{Invariant polynomials}\label{s:invariants}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.26.4}{26.4}.
\leavevmode\label{n:local.binary.form}A binary form $f = \sum_i c_i\, u^i v^{n-i}$ of degree $n$ in the spin kets $u = \jm{1/2}{1/2}$ and $v = \jm{1/2}{-1/2}$ is invariant when $k f = f$ for every element $k$, the elements acting at the parity $+1$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.26.4}{26.4}). At the parity $-1$ the invariants of even degree are the same, and those of odd degree are the semi-invariants of $\det_{\grp{2C_{12h}}}$.
\leavevmode\label{n:function.hilbert}Every spinor irrep $V$ of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ has dimension 1, the group of its matrices on $u$, $v$ being cyclic. Among the finite groups of this catalogue, only $\grp{2O}$, $\grp{2T_{d}}$, $\grp{2O_{h}}$, $\grp{2I}$ and $\grp{2I_{h}}$ have spinor irreps of dimension above 2 [\href{../../../REFERENCES.pdf\#ref.McK80}{McK80}]. The invariants of $V$ are those of the group of its values. \label{n:local.partner.x}The number of linearly independent invariant polynomials of degree $n$ in its partner $x$ is the coefficient of $t^n$ in its Molien series $H_V(t)$ [\href{../../../REFERENCES.pdf\#ref.Mol97}{Mol97}]. When the values are the $m$-th roots of unity, the invariants are the powers of $x^{m}$, and $H_V(t) = 1/(1 - t^{m})$. A level of section~\ref{s:jj} that is two complex-conjugate spinor irreps, of partners $x$ and $y$, has the polynomials in $xy$, $x^{m}$ and $y^{m}$, and $H_V(t) = (1 + t^{m})/((1 - t^{2})(1 - t^{m}))$.
\needspace{84pt}
\paragraph{Binary forms.}
The matrices of the elements on $u$, $v$ form the cyclic group of order 24, whose invariant forms are those of [\href{../../../REFERENCES.pdf\#ref.Kle84}{Kle84}]. In degree $2J$ these forms are the kets at $J$ that $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ leaves invariant [\href{../../../REFERENCES.pdf\#ref.Vil68}{Vil68}, chapter III, section 2]. The number of linearly independent invariant forms of degree $n$ is the coefficient of $t^n$ in $H(t) = (1 + t^{24})/((1-t^{2})(1-t^{24}))$. The invariant forms are the polynomials in $f_1 = uv$, $f_2 = v^{24}$ and $f_3 = u^{24}$, with the one relation $f_{1}^{24} - f_{2}f_{3} = 0$.
A form with $k f = \chi(k) f$ for a one-dimensional irrep $\chi$ is a semi-invariant of $\chi$, and the table lists every such irrep carried by a form, except the trivial one. \label{n:head.generators}The semi-invariants of $\chi$ are the sums of the invariant forms times the generators of its row. Their series is $P_\chi(t)/((1-t^{2})(1-t^{24}))$, with $P_\chi(t) = t^{a} + t^{b}$ for the degrees $a$ and $b$ of the two generators. The element $\elt{\lift{i}}$ leaves every form unchanged, so an irrep with $\chi(\elt{\lift{i}}) = -1$ has no semi-invariants.

\needspace{177pt}
\partbox{Electronic levels}
\section{Spin--orbit levels}\label{s:spin_orbit}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.19}{19} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.2}{25.2}.
\leavevmode\label{n:local.spin}\label{n:symbol.term}A term $\term{2S+1}{\Gamma}$ is the vector irrep $\Gamma$ with the spin $S$, a spin even under the inversion. Under spin--orbit coupling it splits into the irreps of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ in
\begin{equation}\label{e:spinorbit}\restr{(\Gamma \irrprod \irrJp{S}{+1})}{\grp{2C_{12h}}},\end{equation}
and they are spinor irreps at half-integer $S$ and vector irreps at integer $S$. They follow from the products of section~\ref{s:fusion} and the multiplicities of section~\ref{s:subduction_parent}. Row $\Gamma$, column $2S + 1$ of a table gives the levels of $\term{2S+1}{\Gamma}$ for every vector irrep $\Gamma$ and $S = \nicefrac{1}{2}, 1, \dots, \nicefrac{7}{2}$, the nonzero spins of a d or f configuration, and the record holds them (\texttt{spin\symbol{95}orbit\symbol{95}levels}). For an orbital level of section~\ref{s:spin_orbitals}, column $2S + 1 = 2$ gives the levels of one electron, and that section gives their states.

\needspace{91pt}
\section{\texorpdfstring{$jj$}{jj} configurations}\label{s:jj}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.19}{19} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.3}{25.3}.
A spinor level is a Kramers level: a pseudoreal spinor irrep alone, a real one twice or a complex-conjugate pair together. In a level of dimension $d$, $n$ electrons fill its exterior power $\Lambda^{n}$, $n = 0, \dots, d$: vector irreps at even $n$ and spinor irreps at odd $n$. Every level of $\grp{2C_{12h}}$ has dimension 2: $\Lambda^{1}$ is the level, and $\Lambda^{0}$ and $\Lambda^{2}$ are the trivial irrep (\texttt{jj\symbol{95}configurations}).
